LP direction Algorithm:
```
def lp_direction(f, q_limit=None, rem_time):
   f_norm = f / sum(f)
   U=max(f_norm*f_norm)
   f_lp = f_norm + 1e-8*max(f_norm)
   A = conv_matrix(f_lp / U, n, maybe all active)
   c = -ones(n)
   res=linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=ones, bounds=(0,None), method='highs', options={'time_limit':...})
   g=res.x; g=clip_nonneg; if sum>0 normalize.
   return g
```
If no active, use all. To reduce constraints, select active lags where B_k ≥ U*(1-0.05) plus maybe random. But all constraints for n=512 okay. We can do all for n≤512, top for larger.

To compute q accurately and efficiently:
```
def conv_derivative(a, w):
   n=len(a)
   m=len(w) # 2n-1
   N=next_p2(n+m-1)
   ar = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N)
   Wr = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
   h = np.fft.irfft(ar*Wr, N)[:n+m-1]
   h = h[n-1:2*n-1]? Wait earlier h_i = full[n-1:2n-1]. Let's verify with m=2n-1. For i=0, need ∑_{k=0}^{2n-2} a_k w_{k?} with k=0? q1_0 = ∑_{k=0}^{n-1} a_k w_k. Formula h full index n-1 gives ∑ a_k w_{n-1 + (n-1 - k)?} let's recompute carefully.

Let A= a[::-1] (A[r]=a[n-1-r]). W=w length 2n-1. Convolution c[p]=∑_r A[r] W[p-r]. Take p = n-1+i (i=0..n-1): c=∑_r a[n-1-r] W[n-1+i-r]. Let k = n-1-r => r=n-1-k. Then W index = n-1+i - (n-1-k)=i+k. So c=∑_{k=0}^{n-1} a_k W[i+k]. Yes h_i = ∑_{k} a_k w_{i+k}. Good. For q1_i = ∑_k w_k a_{k-i} with valid k-i in [0,n-1]. Let j=k-i => k=j+i, sum a_j w_{i+j}. Same. Good.
   prefix: R_i = sum_{k=i}^{min(i+n-1,2n-2)} w_k. Compute cum = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(w)]). R = cum[i+n-1] - cum[i], with upper clipped. For i=0: R[0]=sum w[0:n-1]. Is that correct? k range i to i+n-1 =0..n-1 (n terms). For i=n-1: R=sum w[n-1..2n-2] (n terms). Good. If w length exactly 2n-1.
   q = h + a*R.
```

### Fast convolution

```
def conv_self(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return empty
    M=2*n-1
    N=1 << (M-1).bit_length()
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    b=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:M]
    # clip tiny negatives due FFT
    b=np.maximum(b,0)
    return b
```
For n large, tiny negative could affect; max okay. But if all zeros? fine.

`score(a)`:
```
n=len(a); S=a.sum(); if S<1e-12 return inf; b=conv_self(a); cmax=max(b,0); return 2*n*cmax/(S*S)
```
Could use evaluate_sequence final.

`normalize(a,target=1.0)`:
```
a=np.asarray(a,float).ravel(); a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0); a=np.clip(a,0,None); S=a.sum(); if S<1e-12: a=np.full_like(a, target/max(1,len(a))); else a=target*a/S; return a
```

`resample(a,n)`: Use np.interp over [0,1]. If n small and a 0? Fine.

`upsample(a,n)`: Could also block repeat: `np.repeat(a, r)[:n]` normalized? For coarse shapes, linear interpolation smoother. We'll use resample (linear) as main; also block maybe for LP candidate.

`soft_weights(d,beta)`:
```
if beta<=0: w=uniform(2n-1)
else:
 z=beta*(d-dmax); z=np.clip(z,-50,0); e=np.exp(z); sum_e=e.sum(); w=e/sum_e
```
But with β huge and all z=-large except max, sum_e~1; okay. If d contains nan, handle.

`corr_pair(a,w)`: length n output h_i = ∑_k a_k w_{i+k}, valid. Implement as previous. Ensure if w length not 2n-1? For active weights length 2n-1. If n=0.
```
def corr_pair(a,w):
  n=len(a); m=len(w)
  if n==0 or m==0: return zeros(n)
  M=n+m-1
  N=next_p2(M)
  A=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],N)
  W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
  full=np.fft.irfft(A*W,N)[:M]
  return full[n-1:n-1+n]  # length n
```
Check for lag single w_s: If w_s=1, n=4, i=1: h=∑_k a_k w_{1+k}; nonzero when k=s-1=2 => h_1=a2. Expected derivative M a at i: a_{s-i}=a2. Good.

`apply_C(w,x)` same as corr_pair.

### Active linear operator and LSQR

Define top-level class? The rule no closures, lambda. Class okay. But LSQR may call `A.shape`, `A.dtype`. Use `scipy.sparse.linalg.AsLinearOperator`? It requires `matvec` callable; lambda problematic. We can define a function that returns a class instance.

```
class ConvWeightedLeastSquares:
    def __init__(self, w, delta=0.0):
       self.w=w; self.n=len(w)//2? w length 2n-1 => n=(len(w)+1)//2; self.delta=delta
    def shape(self): return (self.n,self.n)
    def __matvec__(self, x):
       h=corr_pair(x,self.w)
       return h + self.delta*x
```
But `corr_pair` expects w length 2n-1 and x length n, works.

```
class ConvLinearOperator(sparsity.LinearOperator):
    def __init__(self, w, delta):
       n=(len(w)+1)//2
       super().__init__=('D', (n,n), dtype=np.float64)
       self.w=w; self.delta=delta
    def _matvec(self,x):
       return corr_pair(x,self.w)+self.delta*x
```
This is clean. Import `from scipy.sparse.linalg import LinearOperator, lsqr`. Does `LinearOperator` require `__array__`? okay.

### Weighted L-BFGS for fixed w

Define `weighted_fun(a, w)`:
```
S=sum(a); b=conv_self(a); Q=dot(w,b); score=2*n*Q/(S*S)

# add penalty? We can normalize before, but L-BFGS may drift. Add pen*(S-1)

score += PEN*(S-1)**2

```

### LSE L-BFGS

Define `lse_fun(a,beta)`:
```
S; b; d=n*b/(S*S); dmax; w=softmax(d,beta); L=dot(w,d)
h=corr_pair(a,w)
score=2*L + PEN*(S-1)**2
grad=4*n*h/(S*S) - 4*L/S + 2PEN*(S-1) # because L=w·d. Wait second term 4L/S? Derived: grad=4n h/S^2 -4L/S (yes, L already nQ/S^2). Good.
```
No `Q` factor? Verify with F=2L=2nQ/S^2. grad=4n h/S^2 -4n Q/S^3. L=nQ/S^2 => Q= L S^2/n => second=4L/S. correct.

Use this for smooth global.

### Subgradient/MITIG

### Local polish pipeline

Given an initial normalized array `a0` length n, run:
```
def polish_sequence(a0, deadline, label):
   a=normalize(a0); best=a; bestsc=score(a)
   # Phase 1: active linear LSQR few steps with varying beta

       run minimize(weighted_fun,w) for maybe short
   # Phase 4: local multiplicative fine tune
   for k ... step sizes ...
```

### `lse_fun` details

```
def lse_fun(a, beta):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=len(a)
    S=float(np.sum(a))
    if S < 1e-12:
       return 1e10, np.zeros(n)
    b=conv_self(a)
    d=float(n)*b/(S*S)
    dmax=float(np.max(d)) if d.size else 0
    if not finite: ...
    if beta <= 0: w=np.full(d.size, 1/d.size)
    else:
       z=float(beta)*(d-dmax)
       # For large beta, z <= 0. Clip lower to -50 to avoid underflow; but if beta small fine.
       z=np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
       e=np.exp(z)
       se=e.sum()
       if se<=0: w=uniform; else w=e/se
    L=float(np.dot(w,d))
    L = max(L,0?) d nonneg.
    h=corr_pair(a,w)
    score=2.0*L + PEN*(S-1.0)**2
    grad=(4.0*n*h)/(S*S) - (4.0*L)/S
    grad += 2.0*PEN*(S-1.0)
    # If n? penalty only.
    return score, grad
```
But if beta high and d has large dmax, z clipped to -50 for far lags; okay. If beta = 1e6, z for dmax one 0, others -1e6 clipped -50; sum_e ~ e^{-50} many +1. Weights nearly one-hot but subgradient. L= approx dmax. Good.

Units: dmax ~1, β=500, good.

`weighted_fun`:
```
def weighted_fun(a,w):
   S; b; Q=dot(w,b)
   score=2*n*Q/(S*S) + pen...
   h=corr_pair(a,w)
   grad=4*n*h/(S*S) - 4*n*Q/(S*S*S) + pen...
```
Since second constant not times? correct: -4n Q/S^3. No a. Add penalty.

Check stationary with S=1: grad=4n h -4n Q. If h constant =Q, grad0. Good.

Bounds: For LSE, objective near max d, gradient all zero if w uniform? With high beta, okay.

### Use L-BFGS with objective and gradient returning score+penalty; bounds. For large n, building bounds list in each call expensive? acceptable. We can create `[(0.0, 10.0)]*n` (shared list, okay). But if many calls, maybe memory. Fine.

#### next_power_of_two

def next_power_of_two(m):
    m=int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()

def add_param_start(out, arr, n, limit):
    if len(out)>=limit: return
    a=np.asarray(arr,float).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    if a.sum() <=1e-12: a=np.ones_like(a)
    if a.size != n: a=resample_sequence(a,n)
    out.append(normalize_sequence(a,1.0))
```

#### LP solve
Top-level `sparse_convolution_matrix(f, ks=None, n=None)`:
```
def build_conv_matrix(f, ks):
   n=len(f); rows=len(ks); A=lil_matrix((rows,n))
   for r,k in enumerate(ks):
      start=max(0,k-(n-1)); end=min(k,n-1)
      idx=np.arange(start,end+1)
      if idx.size>0:
         vals=f[k-idx].astype(float)
         vals=np.maximum(vals,0.0)
         A[r, idx]=vals
   return A.tocsr()
```
If ks is None, all range(0,2n-1). For performance for all, use list(range). For n=512 okay. `lil_matrix` assignment with array vals to row slice works.

Let's implement `active_linear_try(a,w,deadline_or_remain, time_limit)`:
```
def active_linear_try(a, w, time_limit, label="lin"):
    a=normalize_sequence(a)
    n=len(a)
    best=a; best_sc=exact_score(a)
    if time_limit<2: return best_sc,best
    # compute row target rhs
    row_sums = row_sums_for_w(w,n)
    target = float(np.mean(row_sums))
    if target <= 1e-12: target=1.0
    rhs = np.full(n, target, dtype=float)
    delta = max(1e-10, 1e-8 * max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(rhs)))))
    op=ConvLinearOperator(w, delta)
    maxiter = int(min(300, max(10, time_limit / max(1e-3, 0.000004*n + 0.003))))
    try:
        sol=lsqr(op, rhs, rtol=1e-8, atol=0.0, maxiter=maxiter)[0]
    except Exception:
        return best_sc,best
    sol=np.nan_to_num(sol, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    sols=[sol]
    # maybe clip negative already in normalize; also negative-renormalize? 
    for sol in ...:
       x=normalize_sequence(np.clip(sol,0,None))
       sc=exact_score(x)
       if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=x
       for t in (0.2,0.5,0.8):
           y=normalize_sequence(t*x+(1-t)*a)
           sc=exact_score(y); update
    return best_sc,best
```
But `sol` from LSQR may have negative; clipping may make poor. Also we can take absolute? No.

Time: LSQR may be slow; but one call. Need compute `row_sums_for_w` top-level:
```
def row_sums_for_w(w,n):
   cum=np.concatenate(([0],np.cumsum(w)))
   idx0=np.arange(0,n)
   idx1=np.minimum(idx0+n-1, 2*n-2)
   return cum[idx1] - cum[idx0]
```
Good.

```
def subgrad_try(a,w,deadline):
   best=a; best_sc=exact_score(a)
   S=sum; h=corr_pair(a,w); Q=dot(w,conv_self(a))
   grad = 4*n*h/(S*S) - 4*n*Q/(S**3)
   # normalize grad magnitude
   gscale=np.max(np.abs(grad)); if 0 return
   for eta in (1e-4,3e-4,1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2,0.1):
      if now>=deadline break
      y=normalize(np.maximum(a - eta*grad/gscale,0)) # if normalized grad scale, eta step ratio
      sc=exact_score(y); update
   return
```
But if grad normalized, eta relative to grad norm. Use `grad = grad/(np.linalg.norm(grad)`). Good.

This may help final.

#### estimate maxfun
```
def estimate_eval_time(n):
    return 0.005 + 0.000004*float(n)
def estimate_maxfun(total, n):
    t=max(1.0,total)
    est=estimate_eval_time(n)
    mf=int(t/(est*1.5)) # one eval per fun
    mf=max(50,min(3000,mf))
    if t<10: mf=80
    return mf
```
L-BFGS `maxfun` counts function evals (objective+grad). We return.

#### run_lse_lbfgs
```
def run_lse_lbfgs(a,beta,maxfun):
    n=len(a)
    if maxfun<30: maxfun=30
    bounds=[(0.0,10.0)]*n
    opts={'ftol':1e-15,'gtol':1e-12,'maxfun':int(maxfun),'maxiter':int(max(40,maxfun//2)),'maxls':30}
    try:
       res=minimize(lse_fun, a, args=(float(beta),), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, bounds=bounds, options=opts)
       x=res.x
    except Exception:
       x=a
    out=normalize_sequence(x)
    sc=exact_score(out)
    return sc,out
```
Maybe bounds upper 1.0 enough but if S penalty drift, 1.0 safe. Use 10.0 to avoid constraints. However L-BFGS may set values huge if objective weird; 10 okay.

`weighted_fun` uses w as args. Since w long, passing each call maybe overhead. But okay. Could preconvert to array.

### Perturb function top-level

```
def perturb_sequence(a, rng, strength=0.2):
    a=normalize(a); n=len(a); mode=rng.integers(0,6)
    if mode0: log noise
    if mode1: blend uniform
    if mode2: reverse
    if mode3: smooth log noise
    if mode4: V-shape multiply
    if mode5: Gaussian smoothing
    return normalize(clip)
```

### Test correctness of gradient geometry

Let's test n=2 with weighted objective single lag. F=2n*b1/S^2 =4*2a0a1/S^2? For a=[0.4,0.6], max? Gradient formula: b1=2a0a1, h=corr_pair(a,w_s), w_s at k=1 -> h=[a1,a0]. Q=b1. S=1. grad=4n h/S^2 -4n Q/S^3 =8[a1,a0] -8[2a0a1,2a0a1]? For a0=.4,a1=.6: grad=[4.8 -3.84, 3.2 -3.84]=[0.96,-0.64]. Direct F=8 a0a1? Actually score=4*2a0a1=8a0a1. dF/da0=8a1=4.8, dF/da1=8a0=3.2. But sum constraint? Our F scale-invariant 8a0a1/S^2; derivative no sum constraint =8(a1/S -2a0a1/S^2?) For S=1: grad=[8a1-16a0a1,8a0-16a0a1]=[4.8-3.84,3.2-3.84] yes. To minimize with sum constraint, projected gradient along direction with zero sum: [0.96,-0.64] sum0.32? Actually sum should be? derivative of scale-invariant F along sum-preserving direction only component orthogonal to 1 matters. Gradient components sum not zero? Sum=0.32. But because denominator derivative constant, gradient includes constant offset; projection subtract mean: mean=0.16, actual [0.8,-0.8] balanced. So gradient formula okay; L-BFGS with bounds and penalty will project implicitly? Pen(s-1) handles sum. The constant offset plus penalty effectively enforces sum. Good.

### Exact score and evaluate_sequence differences with clipping

### LSQR matrix operator consistency

For a sum1, C a = h. In `ConvLinearOperator._matvec`, if x not normalized, h scales. Fine. The LSQR solves min ||C x - rhs||. C not symmetric? It is symmetric but due one-sided `corr_pair`? Does `h_i=∑ a_k w_{i+k}` equal C a for C symmetric? Let's verify C_{i,k}=w_{i+k} if i+k in 0..2n-2, AND also condition k-i? Wait C should have entry w_k if i+k=k? Index careful: Lag = i+j. In `corr_pair(a,w)`, h_i=∑_j a_j w_{i+j}. This corresponds to lag = i+j. Thus operator C_{i,j}=w_{i+j}. This is symmetric in i,j because i+j=j+i. Good. For lag s, C_{i,j}=1 if i+j=s. So yes. Nonzeros for a fixed i: up to n. Symmetric. Good. Row sums prefix. So LSQR okay.

The derivative of Q=∑ w_lag a_i a_j for i+j=lag. If C entries w (offdiag and diag), Q=a^T C a? For offdiag, a^T C a includes 2w a_i a_j, which matches convolution terms. For diagonal (2i=lag), C_ii=w, includes w a_i^2, matches. So Q=a^T C a. Derivative 2C a=2h. Our gradient formulas use h directly as C a, good.

Stationary C a = const, solving C a=rhs. Good.

### Are active weights w over lags summing 1? Then C scale sum w. LSQR rhs target row sums. Good.

Subclass:
```
class ConvLinearOperator(LinearOperator):
    def __init__(self, w, delta=0.0):
        n=(len(w)+1)//2
        super(ConvLinearOperator, self).__init__=('D', (n,n), dtype=np.float64)
        self.w=w
        self.delta=delta
    def _matvec(self, x):
        h=corr_pair(np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64), self.w)
        if self.delta != 0.0:
            h = h + self.delta*x
        return h
```
Maybe `_matvec` must check input dtype. Fine.

def refine_candidate(a0, deadline, time_budget, label):
    a=normalize_sequence(a0)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=exact_score(a)
    n=len(a)
    now=time.monotonic()
    end=min(float(deadline), now + max(10.0, float(time_budget)))
    if n==0: return 1e10, best
    print(...)
    total = end-now
    if total <=20: return best_sc,best
    # linear phase 18%
    lin_end = now + 0.18*total
    # generate weights sequence
    d,b? = d_from_a(a)
    lin_specs=[]
    for beta in (1.0,5.0,20.0,100.0): lin_specs.append(('soft',beta))
    for k in (1,3,8): lin_specs.append(('top',k))
    for spec in lin_specs:
        if now>=lin_end-2: break
        rem_lin = min(lin_end, now + total/20.0) - now? Actually sub_share = (lin_end-now)/len(remaining). Simpler:
        sub_out=min(lin_end, now + max(5, total/20.0))
        w = soft_weights... or topk from d (recompute d maybe)
        sc, cand=active_linear_try(a,w, max(1, sub_out-now))
        if better update; a=normalize(cand) if improved else keep? Update a to cand if cand is not equal? Use if score slightly lower.
        # refresh d maybe every few
    # LSE phase 52%
    lse_end = min(end, now + 0.52*total)
    a=best? Use best as start for LSE? Use current a (best improved). Set a=best.copy().
    lse_betas=(20.0,100.0,400.0,1500.0,6000.0,25000.0,100000.0,300000.0)
    for idx,beta in enumerate(lse_betas):
        if now>=lse_end-10: break
        rem=lse_end-now
        if rem<15: break
        mf=estimate_maxfun(rem*0.6,n)
        sc,a2=run_lse_lbfgs(a,beta,mf)
        if sc<best_sc: update; a=normalize(a2)
        else: a=normalize(a2) # still maybe better for future? If score worse, maybe use previous best? To escape, use a2 if finite? Could degrade. Use if sc<best_sc*1.05? We can set a=normalize(a2) always because LSE result likely smooth; but might worsen exact. For future high beta, local may recover. Use a=normalize(a2) if sc<best_sc+0.05 else keep best? Keep best if bad.
    # weighted phase 15%
    w_end = min(end, now +0.15*total)
    for k in (1,2,3,5,8,16,32):
       if now>=w_end-5: break
       d,_=d_from_a(a)
       w=active_weights_topk(d,k)
       rem=min(w_end, now+30) - now

       update; if sc<best_sc: a=normalize(a2)
    # subgrad 15%
    sg_end = min(end, now+0.15*total)
    for k in (1,2,3,5,8):
       if now>=sg_end-2: break
       d,_=d_from_a(a); w=active_weights_topk(d,k)
       sc,a2=subgrad_try(a,w, min(sg_end, now+20))
       update; if sc<best_sc: a=normalize(a2)
    # final small LSE high beta if time
    if now < end-30:

       mf=estimate_maxfun(rem*0.7,n)
       sc,a2=run_lse_lbfgs(a, max(50000,100000), mf)
       update
    return best_sc,best
```

### We need final answer code only. Ensure no explanation.

Let's write code now in final. Need ensure syntax correct. We'll be careful.

Double-check class import. In SciPy, `LinearOperator` may require `__init__` args. We'll use:
```
class ConvLinearOperator(LinearOperator):
    def __init__(self, w, delta=0.0):
        w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
        n = (w.size + 1) // 2
        super(ConvLinearOperator, self).__init__=('D', (n, n), dtype=np.float64)
        self.w = w
        self.delta = float(delta)
    def _matvec(self, x):
        h = corr_pair(np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64), self.w)
        if self.delta != 0.0:
            h = h + self.delta * np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
        return h
```
If `LinearOperator` import fails, code fails. We'll import with fallback.

`lsqr` may call `_matvec` with x shape (n,). Good.

`active_linear_try`: If `lsqr` returns `sol` with `RunningTime`? okay.

`soft_weights_from_d`: If beta huge and d has large dmax, `z=beta*(d-dmax)` where d-dmax negative maybe -1e-6 * 1e6 = -1, okay. Clip lower -50. But if beta=1e6 and d diff 1e-4, z=-100 clipped -50, many weights not exactly zero; okay.

`exact_score`: Use `conv_self`, but for large sum maybe. `cmax=float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0`.

`d_from_a`: For `d` maybe n=0. Return zeros.

`corr_pair`: For n=1, M=n+m-1=1? w size1, N=1. full[n-1:2n-1]=full[0:1]. okay. For n maybe `a[::-1]` view. Use `np.fft.irfft` output length N; if N=1 maybe RFFT weird? rfft length1 returns shape1; okay.

`weighted_fun`: Need compute `b=conv_self(a); Q=dot(w,b)`. But if w length mismatch, return initial. `h=corr_pair(a,w)`. Score maybe negative due Q? weights nonneg, b nonneg. If S small. Add penalty.

`lse_fun`: `dmax` can be 0. If dmax<=0, w uniform and L=0; h zero; score penalty. Good.

`refine_candidate`: Need refresh `d` in weighted phase. `d_from_a` returns d and S; ignore S.

Potential bug: `subgrad_try` gradient formula: For weighted objective score `F=2nQ/S^2`, grad=4n h/S^2 -4n Q/S^3. If S not1. Then normalize grad to norm1. When doing `a - eta*grad`, because S changes, normalize after. Good.

`perturb_sequence`: Use `rng.standard_normal`. For log noise, `q = a * np.exp(strength * rng.standard_normal(n))`. If a has zeros, multiply zero. Maybe add small eps before log? Multiplicative okay. For smooth noise, use median? Fine.

`make_param_starts`: Need not produce negative; `add_param_start` clips.

`lp_search` in propose: We call `lp_direction` multiple. This function uses `max_n` parameter. We must ensure if input length < max_n, no resample. If length > max_n, resample.

`lp_direction` time_limit may be float; options time_limit int. If time_limit <2, return None to save.

`linprog` may silently take longer than time_limit; options should stop. If no stop, our deadline may be exceeded. But total allow 2400; not fatal if slightly. We can check after each call.

```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t_start=time.monotonic()
    total_budget=float(budget_s)
    deadline=t_start+total_budget-25.0
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    parent=None
    try:
       h=globals().get('height_sequence_1')
       if h is not None:
          p=np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
          p=np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
          p=np.clip(p,0.0,1000.0)
          if p.size>=16:
             parent=p
    except Exception:
       parent=None
    print(...)
    param_n=8192
    high_n=10240 if parent is not None else 8192
    cand_arrays=[]
    if parent is not None:
       cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(parent, high_n))
       cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(parent, 8192))
    param_starts=make_param_starts(param_n, rng, 14)
    cand_arrays.extend(param_starts)
    if parent is not None:
       for a in make_param_starts(high_n, rng, 6): cand_arrays.append(a)
    # coarse LP
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 400.0:
       small_n=512
       lp_end = min(deadline - 800.0, time.monotonic() + 240.0) # reserve? better
       bases=[]
       if parent is not None: bases.append(resample_sequence(parent, small_n))
       for a in param_starts[:8]: bases.append(resample_sequence(a, small_n))
       if not bases: bases.append(np.ones(small_n)/small_n)
       for base in bases:
          now=time.monotonic()
          if now > lp_end - 8.0: break
          cur=normalize_sequence(base)
          for _ in range(2):
             if time.monotonic() > lp_end - 5.0: break
             tl = min(45.0, max(8.0, lp_end - time.monotonic()))
             g=lp_direction(cur, time_limit=tl, max_n=small_n)
             if g is None: break
             cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(g, param_n))
             cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(g, high_n))
             cur=g
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing
    2 * n * max(convolve(a, a)) / sum(a)^2.

This solver combines:
  1) multi-start construction from parent, low-dimensional parametric shapes,
     and small-length LP-driven directions;
  2) active-set linear stationary equations C_w a = const solved with LSQR;
  3) smoothed log-sum-exp L-BFGS-B continuation with an exact active-weight
     gradient;
  4) fixed-active weighted L-BFGS-B polishing;
  5) exact top-k subgradient/mirror perturbation restarts.

The objective is scale-invariant, so candidates are normalized to sum 1.
All heavy operations use FFT-based convolutions/correlations.
"""

import time

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize, linprog
from scipy.sparse import coo_matrix

try:
    from scipy.sparse.linalg import LinearOperator, lsqr
except Exception:
    from scipy.sparse.linalg import lsqr
    from scipy.sparse.linalg.linearoperator import LinearOperator


PEN_SUM = 1e-2


def next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_sequence(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=1e6, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return np.full(a.size, float(target) / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return (float(target) / s) * a


def resample_sequence(a, n):
    a = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel(),
                      nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    n = int(n)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, n)
    if a.size == n:
        return a.copy()
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, max(1, a.size))
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, a).astype(np.float64)


def convolve_self(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    m = 2 * n - 1
    nw = next_power_of_two(m)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, nw)
    b = np.fft.irfft(A * A, nw)[:m]
    return np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)


def exact_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return np.inf
    b = convolve_self(a)
    if b.size == 0:
        return 0.0
    cmax = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(cmax) or cmax < 0.0:
        cmax = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * cmax / (s * s))


def d_from_a(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64), 0.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-15:
        return np.zeros(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64), s
    b = convolve_self(a)
    d = float(n) * b / (s * s)
    return np.nan_to_num(d, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0), s


def corr_pair(a, w):
    """
    One-sided convolution operator C_w a:
        h_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}, with invalid lags ignored.
    This is the matrix C_w whose quadratic form equals sum_lag w_lag b_lag.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    m = int(w.size)
    if n == 0 or m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    full_len = n + m - 1
    nw = next_power_of_two(full_len)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], nw)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, nw)
    full = np.fft.irfft(A * W, nw)[:full_len]
    return np.nan_to_num(full[n - 1:n - 1 + n], nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)


def soft_weights_from_d(d, beta):
    d = np.asarray(d, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(d.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    beta = float(beta)
    if not np.isfinite(beta) or beta <= 1e-8:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    dmax = float(np.max(d))
    if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax <= 0.0:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    z = beta * (d - dmax)
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(z)
    se = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(se) or se <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        imax = int(np.argmax(d))
        w[imax] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / se


def active_weights_topk(d, k):
    d = np.asarray(d, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(d.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if k <= 0 or k >= n:
        return np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    if k == 1:
        idx = int(np.argmax(d))
        w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
        w[idx] = 1.0
        return w
    idx = np.argpartition(d, n - k)[n - k:]
    w = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = 1.0 / float(k)
    return w


def row_sums_for_w(w, n):
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    if n == 0 or w.size == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    pref = np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(w)))
    idx0 = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    idx1 = np.minimum(idx0 + n - 1, w.size - 1)
    return pref[idx1] - pref[idx0]


class ConvLinearOperator(LinearOperator):
    def __init__(self, w, delta=0.0):
        w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        n = (int(w.size) + 1) // 2
        super(ConvLinearOperator, self).__init__(
            'D', (n, n), dtype=np.float64
        )
        self.w = w
        self.delta = float(delta)

    def _matvec(self, x):
        x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        h = corr_pair(x, self.w)
        if self.delta != 0.0:
            h = h + self.delta * x
        return h


def active_linear_try(a, w, time_limit, label="lin"):
    """
    Solve approximately C_w x = target by LSQR, with regularization.
    Try the raw solution, its positive part, its shifted positive part,
    and convex mixes with the current iterate.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if n == 0 or w.size != 2 * n - 1:
        return exact_score(a), a

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)
    time_limit = float(max(0.0, time_limit))
    if time_limit < 3.0:
        return best_sc, best

    ws = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        return best_sc, best
    w = w / ws

    row_sums = row_sums_for_w(w, n)
    target = float(np.mean(row_sums)) if row_sums.size else 1.0
    if not np.isfinite(target) or target <= 1e-12:
        target = 1.0
    rhs = np.full(n, target, dtype=np.float64)
    delta = max(1e-12, 1e-8 * max(1.0, float(np.max(np.abs(rhs)))))

    # One LSQR matrix-vector product costs roughly one FFT.
    iter_est = 0.004 + 0.000005 * float(n)
    maxiter = int(min(420, max(12, time_limit / max(iter_est * 1.5, 1e-5))))

    try:
        op = ConvLinearOperator(w, delta)
        sol = lsqr(op, rhs, rtol=1e-8, atol=0.0, maxiter=maxiter)[0]
    except Exception:
        return best_sc, best

    sol = np.nan_to_num(sol, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)

    candidates = []
    clipped = np.clip(sol, 0.0, None)
    shifted = np.maximum(sol - float(np.min(sol)), 0.0)
    absed = np.abs(sol)
    candidates.append(clipped)
    candidates.append(shifted)
    candidates.append(absed)
    for t in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75):
        candidates.append(t * clipped + (1.0 - t) * a)
        candidates.append(t * shifted + (1.0 - t) * a)
        candidates.append(t * absed + (1.0 - t) * a)

    for cand in candidates:
        cand = normalize_sequence(cand)
        sc = exact_score(cand)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = cand.copy()

    return float(best_sc), best


def weighted_score_grad(a, w):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if s < 1e-10:
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    b = convolve_self(a)
    ws = float(w.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(b.size, 1.0 / max(1, b.size), dtype=np.float64)
    q = float(np.dot(w, b))
    if not np.isfinite(q):
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    score = float(2.0 * n * q / (s * s)) + PEN_SUM * (s - 1.0) ** 2
    h = corr_pair(a, w)
    grad = (4.0 * float(n) * h) / (s * s)
    grad -= (4.0 * float(n) * q) / (s * s * s)
    grad += 2.0 * PEN_SUM * (s - 1.0)
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad = np.clip(grad, -1e6, 1e6)
    return float(score), grad


def run_weighted_lbfgs(a, w, maxfun=300):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    maxfun = int(max(40, min(1200, maxfun)))
    maxiter = int(max(30, maxfun // 2))
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n
    opts = {
        "ftol": 1e-15,
        "gtol": 1e-12,
        "maxfun": maxfun,
        "maxiter": maxiter,
        "maxls": 30,
    }
    sc0 = exact_score(a)
    try:
        res = minimize(
            weighted_score_grad, a, args=(w,),
            method="L-BFGS-B", jac=True, bounds=bounds, options=opts
        )
        x = res.x
    except Exception:
        x = a
    out = normalize_sequence(x)
    sc = exact_score(out)
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc > max(sc0, 1e8):
        return float(sc0), a
    return float(sc), out


def lse_score_grad(a, beta):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(a.size)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if s < 1e-10:
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    b = convolve_self(a)
    d = float(n) * b / (s * s)
    d = np.nan_to_num(d, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    if d.size == 0:
        return 1e10, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    dmax = float(np.max(d))
    if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax <= 0.0:
        return PEN_SUM * (s - 1.0) ** 2, np.full(n, 2.0 * PEN_SUM * (s - 1.0))

    w = soft_weights_from_d(d, float(beta))
    lse = float(np.dot(w, d))
    if not np.isfinite(lse) or lse < 0.0:
        lse = max(dmax, 0.0)

    score = 2.0 * lse + PEN_SUM * (s - 1.0) ** 2
    h = corr_pair(a, w)
    grad = (4.0 * float(n) * h) / (s * s) - (4.0 * lse) / s
    grad += 2.0 * PEN_SUM * (s - 1.0)
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    grad = np.clip(grad, -1e6, 1e6)
    return float(score), grad


def run_lse_lbfgs(a, beta, maxfun=300):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    maxfun = int(max(40, min(1200, maxfun)))
    maxiter = int(max(30, maxfun // 2))
    bounds = [(0.0, 10.0)] * n
    opts = {
        "ftol": 1e-15,
        "gtol": 1e-12,
        "maxfun": maxfun,
        "maxiter": maxiter,
        "maxls": 30,
    }
    sc0 = exact_score(a)
    try:
        res = minimize(
            lse_score_grad, a, args=(float(beta),),
            method="L-BFGS-B", jac=True, bounds=bounds, options=opts
        )
        x = res.x
    except Exception:
        x = a
    out = normalize_sequence(x)
    sc = exact_score(out)
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc > max(sc0, 1e8):
        return float(sc0), a
    return float(sc), out


def estimate_eval_time(n):
    return 0.004 + 0.000004 * float(n)


def estimate_maxfun(total_seconds, n):
    t = float(max(1.0, total_seconds))
    est = estimate_eval_time(n)
    mf = int(t / max(est, 1e-5))
    mf = max(50, mf)
    return min(1200, mf)


def subgrad_try(a, w, deadline):
    """
    Small projected subgradient search for a fixed active weight vector.
    The gradient has a large constant part because the denominator is sum(a)^2;
    we normalize it and try several relative steps.
    """
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)

    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if w.size != 2 * n - 1:
        return best_sc, best

    s = float(a.sum())
    b = convolve_self(a)
    q = float(np.dot(w, b))
    if not np.isfinite(q) or q <= 0.0:
        return best_sc, best
    h = corr_pair(a, w)
    grad = (4.0 * float(n) * h) / (s * s) - (4.0 * float(n) * q) / (s * s * s)
    grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    gnorm = float(np.linalg.norm(grad))
    if not np.isfinite(gnorm) or gnorm <= 1e-15:
        return best_sc, best
    grad = grad / gnorm

    etas = (0.001, 0.003, 0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3)
    for eta in etas:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        x = np.maximum(a - float(eta) * grad, 0.0)
        x = normalize_sequence(x)
        sc = exact_score(x)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = x.copy()
    return float(best_sc), best


def add_param_start(out, arr, n, limit):
    if len(out) >= int(limit):
        return
    arr = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if arr.size == 0:
        return
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 1e-15:
        arr = np.ones_like(arr)
    if arr.size != n:
        arr = resample_sequence(arr, n)
    out.append(normalize_sequence(arr, 1.0))


def make_param_starts(n, rng):
    out = []
    n = int(n)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

    for base in (0.2, 0.5, 0.8, 1.2, 1.8, 2.5):
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + base * x, n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + base * (1.0 - x), n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + base * np.abs(2.0 * x - 1.0), n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + base * (2.0 * x - 1.0) ** 2, n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + base * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x), n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + base * np.cos(3.0 * np.pi * x), n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, np.exp(-2.0 * x) + np.exp(-2.0 * (1.0 - x)), n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, x ** 0.4 + (1.0 - x) ** 0.4, n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, x ** 2.0 + (1.0 - x) ** 5.0, n, 80)
        add_param_start(out, (1.0 - x) ** 2.0 + x ** 5.0, n, 80)

    # Fourier smooth random starts.
    nw = next_power_of_two(n)
    freqs = np.fft.rfftfreq(nw) * nw
    for _ in range(10):
        p = float(rng.uniform(1.2, 3.5))
        power = 1.0 / ((1.0 + np.abs(freqs)) ** p)
        power[0] = 0.0
        phase = rng.uniform(-np.pi, np.pi, size=power.size)
        spec = power * np.exp(1j * phase)
        noise = np.fft.irfft(spec, nw)[:n]
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.25, 1.2))
        add_param_start(out, 1.0 + amp * (noise - float(noise.mean())), n, 80)

    # Random piecewise-linear starts.
    for _ in range(8):
        k = int(rng.integers(3, 8))
        knots = np.sort(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.95, size=k))
        vals = np.exp(rng.uniform(-0.6, 1.2, size=k))
        arr = np.interp(x, knots, vals)
        add_param_start(out, arr, n, 80)

    # Random piecewise-constant block starts.
    for _ in range(10):
        nb = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
        widths = rng.dirichlet(np.ones(nb))
        centers = np.cumsum(widths)
        centers[-1] = 1.0
        amps = np.exp(rng.uniform(-0.8, 1.5, size=nb))
        idx = np.searchsorted(centers, x, side="right")
        idx = np.clip(idx, 0, nb - 1)
        arr = amps[idx] / max(1.0, float(amps.sum()))
        add_param_start(out, arr, n, 80)

    return out


def build_convolution_constraint_matrix(f, ks):
    n = int(f.size)
    rows = []
    cols = []
    vals = []
    for r, k in enumerate(ks):
        start = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        end = min(k, n - 1)
        idx = np.arange(start, end + 1)
        if idx.size == 0:
            continue
        v = np.asarray(f[k - idx], dtype=np.float64)
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
        rows.append(np.full(idx.size, int(r), dtype=np.int32))
        cols.append(idx.astype(np.int32, copy=False))
        vals.append(v)
    if not rows:
        return coo_matrix((len(ks), n), dtype=np.float64).tocsr()
    rows = np.concatenate(rows)
    cols = np.concatenate(cols)
    vals = np.concatenate(vals)
    A = coo_matrix((vals, (rows, cols)), shape=(len(ks), n), dtype=np.float64)
    return A.tocsc()


def lp_direction(f, time_limit=20.0, max_n=512, upper_bound=100.0):
    """
    SoTA-inspired LP direction:
      maximize sum(x) subject to x>=0 and f*x <= 1
    where f is a nonnegative current sequence scaled by its current self-conv peak.
    This is feasible and bounded because of the explicit upper bound.
    """
    f = normalize_sequence(f)
    n = int(f.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return None
    time_limit = float(max(0.0, time_limit))
    if time_limit < 8.0:
        return None
    if n > int(max_n):
        f = resample_sequence(f, int(max_n))
        n = int(f.size)

    b = convolve_self(f)
    u = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(u) or u <= 1e-15:
        return None

    f_safe = f + 1e-8 * max(1.0, float(np.max(f)))
    f_safe = np.maximum(f_safe, 1e-12)
    f_scaled = f_safe / u

    ks = np.arange(0, 2 * n, dtype=np.int64)
    try:
        A = build_convolution_constraint_matrix(f_scaled, ks)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if A.nnz <= 0:
        return None

    c = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    b_ub = np.ones(2 * n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds = [(0.0, float(upper_bound))] * n
    opts = {
        "time_limit": int(max(1, time_limit)),
        "disp": False,
        "presolve": True,
    }
    try:
        res = linprog(
            c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds,
            method="highs", options=opts
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            res = linprog(
                c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method="highs"
            )
        except Exception:
            return None

    if res is None or getattr(res, "x", None) is None:
        return None
    if int(getattr(res, "status", 1)) != 0:
        return None

    g = np.nan_to_num(np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).ravel(),
                      nan=0.0, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    g = np.maximum(g, 0.0)
    if float(g.sum()) <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return normalize_sequence(g, 1.0)


def perturb_sequence(a, rng, strength=0.2):
    a = normalize_sequence(a)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    strength = float(max(0.0, min(1.0, strength)))
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 6))

    if mode == 0:
        q = a * np.exp(strength * 0.35 * rng.standard_normal(n))
    elif mode == 1:
        t = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.25))
        q = (1.0 - t) * a + t * (np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n)
    elif mode == 2:
        q = a[::-1].copy()
        q = q * np.exp(0.08 * rng.standard_normal(n))
    elif mode == 3:
        loga = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-12))
        noise = rng.standard_normal(n)
        k = int(rng.integers(5, max(7, min(n, n // 200) + 1)))
        if k < 3:
            k = 3
        if k % 2 == 0:
            k += 1
        if k > n:
            k = n if n % 2 == 1 else n - 1
        if k < 3:
            k = 3
        c = k // 2
        sig = max(1.0, c / 2.0)
        kern = np.exp(-((np.arange(k) - c) ** 2) / (2.0 * sig * sig))
        kern = kern / float(kern.sum())
        noise = np.convolve(noise, kern, mode="same")
        noise = noise - float(noise.mean())
        q = np.exp(loga + strength * 0.35 * noise - float(np.max(loga)))
    elif mode == 4:
        xv = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
        shp = 1.0 + 0.5 * strength * np.abs(2.0 * xv - 1.0)
        q = a * shp
    else:
        k = int(rng.integers(5, max(7, min(n, n // 200) + 1)))
        if k < 3:
            k = 3
        if k % 2 == 0:
            k += 1
        if k > n:
            k = n if n % 2 == 1 else n - 1
        c = k // 2
        sig = max(1.0, c / 2.0)
        kern = np.exp(-((np.arange(k) - c) ** 2) / (2.0 * sig * sig))
        kern = kern / float(kern.sum())
        q = np.convolve(a, kern, mode="same")

    q = np.nan_to_num(q, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return normalize_sequence(np.maximum(q, 0.0), 1.0)


def refine_candidate(a0, deadline, time_budget, label="cand"):
    a = normalize_sequence(a0)
    n = int(a.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)

    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = exact_score(a)
    now = time.monotonic()
    end = min(float(deadline), now + float(max(15.0, time_budget)))
    total = max(0.0, end - now)
    print("Refine %s len=%d start=%.8f budget=%.1fs" % (
        label, n, best_sc, total
    ))

    if total < 20.0:
        return float(best_sc), best

    # 1. Active-set linear stationary solves.
    lin_end = now + 0.18 * total
    lin_specs = []
    for beta in (1.0, 5.0, 20.0, 100.0):
        lin_specs.append(("soft", beta))
    for k in (1, 3, 8):
        lin_specs.append(("top", float(k)))

    for idx, spec in enumerate(lin_specs):
        if time.monotonic() >= lin_end - 2.0:
            break
        d, _ = d_from_a(a)
        if spec[0] == "soft":
            w = soft_weights_from_d(d, spec[1])
        else:
            w = active_weights_topk(d, int(spec[1]))
        share = max(4.0, (lin_end - time.monotonic()) / max(1, len(lin_specs) - idx))
        sub_dead = min(lin_end, time.monotonic() + share)
        sc, cand = active_linear_try(a, w, max(1.0, sub_dead - time.monotonic()), "lin")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = cand.copy()
    a = normalize_sequence(best)
    print("After linear active-set: %.8f" % best_sc)

    # 2. LSE L-BFGS-B continuation.
    lse_end = now + 0.52 * total
    lse_betas = (20.0, 100.0, 400.0, 1500.0, 6000.0, 25000.0, 100000.0, 300000.0)
    a = normalize_sequence(best)
    for beta in lse_betas:
        if time.monotonic() >= lse_end - 10.0:
            break
        rem = lse_end - time.monotonic()
        if rem <= 12.0:
            break
        maxfun = estimate_maxfun(rem * 0.6, n)
        sc, a2 = run_lse_lbfgs(a, beta, maxfun)
        a2 = normalize_sequence(a2)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = a2.copy()
        # Continue from the smoothed point only if it is not badly worse.
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc + 0.25:
            a = a2
        else:
            a = normalize_sequence(best)

    print("After LSE: %.8f" % best_sc)

    # 3. Fixed active weighted L-BFGS-B.
    w_end = now + 0.15 * total
    a = normalize_sequence(best)
    for k in (1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 16, 32):
        if time.monotonic() >= w_end - 5.0:
            break
        d, _ = d_from_a(a)
        w = active_weights_topk(d, k)
        rem = min(w_end, time.monotonic() + 30.0) - time.monotonic()
        if rem <= 8.0:
            break
        maxfun = estimate_maxfun(rem * 0.8, n)
        sc, a2 = run_weighted_lbfgs(a, w, maxfun)
        a2 = normalize_sequence(a2)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = a2.copy()
            a = a2

    print("After weighted active: %.8f" % best_sc)

    # 4. Exact top-k subgradient nudges.
    sg_end = now + 0.15 * total
    a = normalize_sequence(best)
    for k in (1, 2, 3, 5, 8):
        if time.monotonic() >= sg_end - 2.0:
            break
        d, _ = d_from_a(a)
        w = active_weights_topk(d, k)
        sub_dead = min(sg_end, time.monotonic() + 18.0)
        sc, a2 = subgrad_try(a, w, sub_dead)
        a2 = normalize_sequence(a2)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best = a2.copy()
            a = a2

    # 5. Final short high-beta LSE polish.
    if time.monotonic() < end - 25.0:
        a = normalize_sequence(best)
        rem = end - time.monotonic()
        if rem > 30.0:
            maxfun = estimate_maxfun(rem * 0.7, n)
            sc, a2 = run_lse_lbfgs(a, 150000.0, maxfun)
            a2 = normalize_sequence(a2)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = float(sc)
                best = a2.copy()

    print("Refine done %s final=%.8f len=%d" % (label, best_sc, n))
    return float(best_sc), best


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = float(budget_s)
    deadline = t_start + total_budget - 25.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    parent = None
    try:
        h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
        if h is not None:
            p = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
            p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
            if p.size >= 16:
                parent = p
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    print("Parent available: %s, len=%d" % (
        parent is not None,
        parent.size if parent is not None else 0
    ))

    high_n = 10240 if parent is not None else 8192
    mid_n = 8192

    # Initial candidate pool.
    cand_arrays = []
    if parent is not None:
        cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(parent, high_n))
        cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(parent, mid_n))

    param_starts = make_param_starts(mid_n, rng)
    for arr in param_starts:
        cand_arrays.append(arr)
    if parent is not None:
        for arr in make_param_starts(high_n, rng)[:6]:
            cand_arrays.append(arr)

    # Coarse LP-driven exploration.
    if deadline - time.monotonic() > 450.0:
        small_n = 512
        lp_end = min(deadline - 900.0, time.monotonic() + 260.0)
        bases = []
        if parent is not None:
            bases.append(resample_sequence(parent, small_n))
        for arr in param_starts[:8]:
            bases.append(resample_sequence(arr, small_n))
        if not bases:
            bases.append(np.ones(small_n, dtype=np.float64) / small_n)

        for base in bases:
            now = time.monotonic()
            if now > lp_end - 10.0:
                break
            cur = normalize_sequence(base)
            for _ in range(2):
                now = time.monotonic()
                if now > lp_end - 6.0:
                    break
                tl = min(45.0, max(8.0, lp_end - now))
                g = lp_direction(cur, time_limit=tl, max_n=small_n)
                if g is None:
                    break
                if g.size > 0:
                    cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(g, mid_n))
                    cand_arrays.append(resample_sequence(g, high_n))
                    cur = g
                else:
                    break

    # Evaluate and rank initial candidates.
    scored = []
    for i, arr in enumerate(cand_arrays):
        try:
            a = normalize_sequence(np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel())
            if a.size < 8:
                continue
            sc = exact_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                scored.append((float(sc), int(i), a))
        except Exception:
            continue

    if not scored:
        fallback = normalize_sequence(np.ones(max(1, high_n), dtype=np.float64), 1.0)
        scored = [(float(exact_score(fallback)), 0, fallback)]

    scored.sort()
    print("Initial top scores: " + ", ".join(
        "%.8f(len=%d)" % (s, a.size) for s, _, a in scored[:6]
    ))

    best_sc = float(scored[0][0])
    best_seq = scored[0][2].copy()

    # Select a few distinct top candidates.
    top_candidates = []
    for sc, _, arr in scored[:10]:
        if len(top_candidates) >= 3:
            break
        dup = False
        for other in top_candidates:
            if arr.size == other.size and np.allclose(arr, other, rtol=0.0, atol=1e-9):
                dup = True
                break
        if not dup:
            top_candidates.append(arr.copy())
    if not top_candidates:
        top_candidates.append(best_seq.copy())

    # Main polishing budgets.
    for j, arr in enumerate(top_candidates):
        rem = float(deadline - time.monotonic())
        if rem < 130.0:
            break
        if j == 0:
            budget = min(1400.0, rem - 650.0)
        elif j == 1:
            budget = min(420.0, rem - 420.0)
        else:
            budget = min(260.0, rem - 260.0)
        budget = max(60.0, budget)
        budget = min(budget, max(30.0, rem - 30.0))
        sc, a2 = refine_candidate(arr, deadline, budget, "top%d" % j)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best_seq = a2.copy()
            print("New best after top%d: %.8f len=%d" % (j, best_sc, best_seq.size))

    # Optional upscale to a larger length.
    if best_seq.size < 10240:
        targets = []
        for target in (8192, 10240, 12288):
            if target > best_seq.size:
                targets.append(target)
        for target in targets:
            rem = float(deadline - time.monotonic())
            if rem < 300.0:
                break
            up = resample_sequence(best_seq, target)
            sc = exact_score(up)
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                continue
            # Polish if it is competitive; small upsample losses are often recoverable.
            if sc <= best_sc * 1.04:
                budget = min(420.0, rem - 120.0)
                budget = max(90.0, budget)
                sc, a2 = refine_sequence=None  # placeholder to be replaced below
                # NOTE: use refine_candidate directly.
                sc, a2 = refine_candidate(up, deadline, budget, "upscale%d" % target)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = float(sc)
                    best_seq = a2.copy()
                    print("New best after upscale to %d: %.8f" % (target, best_sc))
                if sc < best_sc:
                    break

    # Perturbation restarts from the current best.
    perturb_count = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline - 160.0 and perturb_count < 4:
        rem = float(deadline - time.monotonic())
        if rem < 180.0:
            break
        budget = min(240.0, rem / max(1, (4 - perturb_count)) - 30.0)
        if budget < 70.0:
            break
        p = perturb_sequence(best_seq, rng, strength=float(rng.uniform(0.08, 0.3)))
        sc, a2 = refine_candidate(p, deadline, budget, "perturb%d" % perturb_count)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best_seq = a2.copy()
            print("New best after perturb%d: %.8f len=%d" % (perturb_count, best_sc, best_seq.size))
        perturb_count += 1

    # Final short polish.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 60.0:
        budget = min(140.0, max(40.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 35.0))
        sc, a2 = refine_candidate(best_seq, deadline, budget, "final")
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = float(sc)
            best_seq = a2.copy()

    # Final sanitization and evaluated report.
    out_arr = np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    out_arr = np.nan_to_num(out_arr, nan=0.0, posinf=1000.0, neginf=0.0)
    out_arr = np.clip(out_arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
    out_arr = normalize_sequence(out_arr, 1.0)

    if out_arr.size == 0 or float(out_arr.sum()) < 0.01:
        out_arr = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5

    out = [float(x) for x in out_arr]
    try:
        verified = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
    except Exception:
        verified = float(best_sc)

    print("FINAL: internal=%.10f evaluated=%.10f len=%d time=%.1fs" % (
        best_sc, verified, len(out), time.monotonic() - t_start
    ))
    return out
```
